# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. }) => any;

Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. type find_similar_molecules = (_: {

}) => any;

}) => any;

type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {

expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. type get_electronic_properties = (_: {

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

smiles: string
}) => any;

Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. }) => any;

type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

}) => any;

type get_electronic_properties = (_: {

**Reasoning**  
1. **Molecular weight (MW)** – ≈ 260 Da (well below the 500 Da threshold often used for CNS agents).  
2. **Lipinski‑like filters** –  
   - H‑bond donors (HBD) = 0 (≤ 1).  
   - H‑bond acceptors (HBA) = 4 (≤ 7).  
   - Rotatable bonds ≈ 1–2 (≤ 3).  
3. **Topological Polar Surface Area (tPSA)** – about 50 Å² (from one carbonyl, one methoxy O, and two nitro O’s) which is below the ≈ 90 Å² cut‑off that typically favors BBB penetration.  
4. **LogP** – The phenyl ring and imide moiety provide sufficient lipophilicity (logP ≈ 2.5‑3.5), balancing aqueous solubility with membrane permeability.  
5. **Charge state** – The compound is neutral at physiological pH (no ionizable groups).  

All these physicochemical descriptors fall within the ranges that favor blood‑brain barrier (BBB) permeability. Consequently, the drug is predicted to **cross the BBB**.<|return|>

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

**Rationale**  

1. **Physicochemical profile**  
   - *Molecular weight:* 325 Da (≤ 400)  
   - *logP:* 2.4 (moderately lipophilic)  
   - *Topological polar surface area (TPSA):* 85 Å² (< 90)  
   - *H‑bond donors:* 0, *acceptors:* 5 (within limits)  
   - *Rotatable bonds:* 4 (moderate flexibility)  
   - *No ionizable groups at pH 7.4* – neutral, no positive/negative charges that would hinder passive diffusion.

2. **Rule‑of‑thumb for BBB penetration**  
   - Lipophilicity between 2–5, TPSA < 90 Å², HBD ≤1, MW < 400 → high likelihood of passive diffusion across the BBB.

3. **Functional groups**  
   - Contains a nitro group, an imide, and a phenyl ring – none of which impose steric or polar burdens that preclude BBB crossing.  
   - Toxicophore analysis flagged a nitro group and other reactive motifs, but these do not negate membrane permeability.

4. **ADME assessment** (pH 7.4)  
   - *LogD* = 2.38 (consistent with favorable permeability).  
   - *Net charge:* neutral (zwitterionic internal charges cancel out).  
   - *Solubility* logS ≈ –4.8 (low but still acceptable for a drug with moderate lipophilicity).

5. **Conclusion**  
   The compound satisfies the key physicochemical criteria predictive of blood‑brain barrier penetration and lacks features that would prevent crossing. Therefore, the most reasonable prediction is that it *crosses* the BBB.<|return|>

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).

// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

smiles: string
}) => any;

// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {

smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. type screen_toxicophores = (_: {

}) => any;

// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {

type get_electronic_properties = (_: {

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

- **QED:** 0.54 – respectable drug‑likeness score.  
- **Net charge at pH 7.4:** –4 (four ionized phosphates).  

**Step 2 – Functional‑Group Screening**  
- The molecule contains two **phosphoryl (P=O)** groups and a thioether‑connected chlorophenyl ring.  
- **Toxicophore alerts**:  
  - *Reactive phosphorus groups* – can alkylate biological nucleophiles.  
  - *Halogenated aromatic ring* – often associated with non‑genotoxic carcinogenicity and acute aquatic toxicity.  
  - *High density of negative charges* – typical of organophosphates that interfere with cholinesterase or are electrophilic toward proteins.  

**Step 3 – ADME & Permeability**  
- **LogD at pH 7.4:** –6.86 → extremely low membrane permeability.  
- **pKa of acid sites:** ~1 → fully ionized under physiological conditions.  
- These properties explain why the compound is unlikely to be orally bioavailable, but they do not eliminate its potential for acute toxicity when exposed through other routes (e.g., inhalation, dermal).  

**Step 4 – Metabolic Considerations**  
- Organophosphates (e.g., parathion, chlorpyrifos) share a P=O core and are notorious AChE inhibitors.  
- The thioether and chlorobenzene motif strongly resemble known pesticides rather than approved drugs.  

**Step 5 – Conclusion**  
The structural pattern and toxicophore alerts strongly align with known **organophosphate‑type toxicities** (AChE inhibition, acute cholinergic crisis). No FDA‑approved drug matches this scaffold; instead, it is a classic pesticide chemistry motif.

**Final Prediction:** **(B) toxic**.  

**Answer:** (B)<|return|>

smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

expression: string
}) => any;

Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
